Player_White (to Proxy):

An analysis of the current position reveals that Black's pawn on b3 is a major nuisance, being deep in your territory and restricting your b2 rook. However, right now this pawn is firmly defended by Black's light-squared bishop on e6 along the e6-b3 diagonal. 

We can use a classic tactical motif known as **interference** to break this defense! 

By pushing your pawn to c4 (`c3c4`), you place it squarely on the diagonal between the e6 bishop and the b3 pawn. 
- The c4 square is protected by your knight on b6.
- If Black plays `Bxc4` to eliminate the pawn, you recapture with `Nxc4`, winning a bishop for a pawn. 
- If Black ignores it, the bishop's line of sight is permanently blocked, and the b3 pawn is left totally undefended for your b2 rook to gobble up on the next turn.

make_move c3c4
